{"parentUuid":"5a109338-cdb6-4294-8a1c-97598412412b","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a2d6da1c26e592eaa","attachment":{"type":"skill_listing","content":"- heartbeat-protocol: Claude node contract for DebateAI Graph Spine v2. The Main Orchestrator (Claude-Router seat) contract; thin loader over the repo spine. All agent launches use /goal.\n- commit-commands:clean_gone: Cleans up all git branches marked as [gone] (branches that have been deleted on the remote but still exist locally), including removing associated worktrees.\n- commit-commands:commit-push-pr: Commit, push, and open a PR\n- commit-commands:commit: Create a git commit\n- pdf-viewer:annotate: Collaboratively annotate a PDF — propose markup, review together, iterate\n- pdf-viewer:fill-form: Fill PDF form fields interactively with live visual feedback\n- pdf-viewer:open: Open a PDF in the interactive viewer\n- pdf-viewer:sign: Place a signature or initials image on a PDF\n- frontend-design:frontend-design: Guidance for distinctive, intentional visual design when building new UI or reshaping an existing one. Helps with aesthetic direction, typography, and making choices that don't read as templated defaults.\n- mattpocock-skills:diagnosing-bugs: Diagnosis loop for hard bugs and performance regressions. Use when the user says \"diagnose\"/\"debug this\", or reports something broken/throwing/failing/slow.\n- mattpocock-skills:tdd: Test-driven development. Use when the user wants to build features or fix bugs test-first, mentions \"red-green-refactor\", or wants integration tests.\n- mattpocock-skills:prototype: Build a throwaway prototype to answer a design question. Use when the user wants to sanity-check whether a state model or logic feels right, or explore what a UI should look like.\n- mattpocock-skills:research: Investigate a question against high-trust primary sources and capture the findings as a Markdown file in the repo. Use when the user wants a topic researched, docs or API facts gathered, or reading legwork delegated to a background agent.\n- mattpocock-skills:domain-modeling: Build and sharpen a project's domain model. Use when the user wants to pin down domain terminology or a ubiquitous language, record an architectural decision, or when another skill needs to maintain the domain model.\n- mattpocock-skills:codebase-design: Shared vocabulary for designing deep modules. Use when the user wants to design or improve a module's interface, find deepening opportunities, decide where a seam goes, make code more testable or AI-navigable, or when another skill needs the deep-module vocabulary.\n- mattpocock-skills:code-review: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes — Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to \"review since X\".\n- mattpocock-skills:resolving-merge-conflicts: Use when you need to resolve an in-progress git merge/rebase conflict.\n- mattpocock-skills:grilling: Grill the user relentlessly about a plan, decision, or idea. Use when the user wants to stress-test their thinking, or uses any 'grill' trigger phrases.\n- superpowers:brainstorming: You MUST use this before any creative work - creating features, building components, adding functionality, or modifying behavior. Explores user intent, requirements and design before implementation.\n- superpowers:dispatching-parallel-agents: Use when facing 2+ independent tasks that can be worked on without shared state or sequential dependencies\n- superpowers:executing-plans: Use when you have a written implementation plan to execute in a separate session with review checkpoints\n- superpowers:finishing-a-development-branch: Use when implementation is complete, all tests pass, and you need to decide how to integrate the work\n- superpowers:receiving-code-review: Use when receiving code review feedback, before implementing suggestions, especially if feedback seems unclear or technically questionable - requires technical rigor and verification, not performative agreement or blind implementation\n- superpowers:requesting-code-review: Use when completing tasks, implementing major features, or before merging to verify work meets requirements\n- superpowers:subagent-driven-development: Use when executing implementation plans with independent tasks in the current session\n- superpowers:systematic-debugging: Use when encountering any bug, test failure, or unexpected behavior, before proposing fixes\n- superpowers:test-driven-development: Use when implementing any feature or bugfix, before writing implementation code\n- superpowers:using-git-worktrees: Use when starting feature work that needs isolation from current workspace or before executing implementation plans - ensures an isolated workspace exists via native tools or git worktree fallback\n- superpowers:using-superpowers: Use when starting any conversation - establishes how to find and use skills, requiring skill invocation before ANY response including clarifying questions\n- superpowers:verification-before-completion: Use when about to claim work is complete, fixed, or passing, before committing or creating PRs - requires running verification commands and confirming output before making any success claims; evidence before assertions always\n- superpowers:writing-plans: Use when you have a spec or requirements for a multi-step task, before touching code\n- superpowers:writing-skills: Use when creating new skills, editing existing skills, or verifying skills work before deployment\n- pdf-viewer:view-pdf: Interactive PDF viewer. Use when the user wants to open, show, or view a PDF and collaborate on it visually — annotate, highlight, stamp, fill form fields, place signature/initials, or review markup together. Not for summarization or text extraction (use native Read instead).\n- productivity:memory-management: Two-tier memory system that makes Claude a true workplace collaborator. Decodes shorthand, acronyms, nicknames, and internal language so Claude understands requests like a colleague would. CLAUDE.md for working memory, memory/ directory for the full knowledge base.\n- productivity:start: Initialize the productivity system and open the dashboard. Use when setting up the plugin for the first time, bootstrapping working memory from your existing task list, or decoding the shorthand (nicknames, acronyms, project codenames) you use in your todos.\n- productivity:task-management: Simple task management using a shared TASKS.md file. Reference this when the user asks about their tasks, wants to add/complete tasks, or needs help tracking commitments.\n- productivity:update: Sync tasks and refresh memory from your current activity. Use when pulling new assignments from your project tracker into TASKS.md, triaging stale or overdue tasks, filling memory gaps for unknown people or projects, or running a comprehensive scan to catch todos buried in chat and email.\n- cowork-plugin-management:cowork-plugin-customizer: Customize a Claude Code plugin for a specific organization's tools and workflows. Use when: customize plugin, set up plugin, configure plugin, tailor plugin, adjust plugin settings, customize plugin connectors, customize plugin skill, tweak plugin, modify plugin configuration.\n- cowork-plugin-management:create-cowork-plugin: Guide users through creating a new plugin from scratch in a cowork session. Use when users want to create a plugin, build a plugin, make a new plugin, develop a plugin, scaffold a plugin, start a plugin from scratch, or design a plugin. This skill requires Cowork mode with access to the outputs directory for delivering the final .plugin file.\n- engineering:architecture: Create or evaluate an architecture decision record (ADR). Use when choosing between technologies (e.g., Kafka vs SQS), documenting a design decision with trade-offs and consequences, reviewing a system design proposal, or designing a new component from requirements and constraints.\n- engineering:code-review: Review code changes for security, performance, and correctness. Trigger with a PR URL or diff, \"review this before I merge\", \"is this code safe?\", or when checking a change for N+1 queries, injection risks, missing edge cases, or error handling gaps.\n- engineering:debug: Structured debugging session — reproduce, isolate, diagnose, and fix. Trigger with an error message or stack trace, \"this works in staging but not prod\", \"something broke after the deploy\", or when behavior diverges from expected and the cause isn't obvious.\n- engineering:deploy-checklist: Pre-deployment verification checklist. Use when about to ship a release, deploying a change with database migrations or feature flags, verifying CI status and approvals before going to production, or documenting rollback triggers ahead of time.\n- engineering:documentation: Write and maintain technical documentation. Trigger with \"write docs for\", \"document this\", \"create a README\", \"write a runbook\", \"onboarding guide\", or when the user needs help with any form of technical writing — API docs, architecture docs, or operational runbooks.\n- engineering:incident-response: Run an incident response workflow — triage, communicate, and write postmortem. Trigger with \"we have an incident\", \"production is down\", an alert that needs severity assessment, a status update mid-incident, or when writing a blameless postmortem after resolution.\n- engineering:standup: Generate a standup update from recent activity. Use when preparing for daily standup, summarizing yesterday's commits and PRs and ticket moves, formatting work into yesterday/today/blockers, or structuring a few rough notes into a shareable update.\n- engineering:system-design: Design systems, services, and architectures. Trigger with \"design a system for\", \"how should we architect\", \"system design for\", \"what's the right architecture for\", or when the user needs help with API design, data modeling, or service boundaries.\n- engineering:tech-debt: Identify, categorize, and prioritize technical debt. Trigger with \"tech debt\", \"technical debt audit\", \"what should we refactor\", \"code health\", or when the user asks about code quality, refactoring priorities, or maintenance backlog.\n- engineering:testing-strategy: Design test strategies and test plans. Trigger with \"how should we test\", \"test strategy for\", \"write tests for\", \"test plan\", \"what tests do we need\", or when the user needs help with testing approaches, coverage, or test architecture.\n- design:accessibility-review: Run a WCAG 2.1 AA accessibility audit on a design or page. Trigger with \"audit accessibility\", \"check a11y\", \"is this accessible?\", or when reviewing a design for color contrast, keyboard navigation, touch target size, or screen reader behavior before handoff.\n- design:design-critique: Get structured design feedback on usability, hierarchy, and consistency. Trigger with \"review this design\", \"critique this mockup\", \"what do you think of this screen?\", or when sharing a Figma link or screenshot for feedback at any stage from exploration to final polish.\n- design:design-handoff: Generate developer handoff specs from a design. Use when a design is ready for engineering and needs a spec sheet covering layout, design tokens, component props, interaction states, responsive breakpoints, edge cases, and animation details.\n- design:design-system: Audit, document, or extend your design system. Use when checking for naming inconsistencies or hardcoded values across components, writing documentation for a component's variants, states, and accessibility notes, or designing a new pattern that fits the existing system.\n- design:research-synthesis: Synthesize user research into themes, insights, and recommendations. Use when you have interview transcripts, survey results, usability test notes, support tickets, or NPS responses that need to be distilled into patterns, user segments, and prioritized next steps.\n- design:user-research: Plan, conduct, and synthesize user research. Trigger with \"user research plan\", \"interview guide\", \"usability test\", \"survey design\", \"research questions\", or when the user needs help with any aspect of understanding their users through research.\n- design:ux-copy: Write or review UX copy — microcopy, error messages, empty states, CTAs. Trigger with \"write copy for\", \"what should this button say?\", \"review this error message\", or when naming a CTA, wording a confirmation dialog, filling an empty state, or writing onboarding text.\n- anthropic-skills:consolidate-memory: Reflective pass over your memory files — merge duplicates, fix stale facts, prune the index.\n- anthropic-skills:docx: Use this skill whenever the user wants to create, read, edit, or manipulate Word documents (.docx files) or Word templates (.dotx files). Triggers include: any mention of 'Word doc', 'word document', '.docx', '.dotx', or requests to produce professional documents with formatting like tables of contents, headings, page numbers, or letterheads. Also use when extracting or reorganizing content from .docx or .dotx files, inserting or replacing images in documents, performing find-and-replace in Word files, working with tracked changes or comments, or converting content into a polished Word document. If the user asks for a 'report', 'memo', 'letter', 'template', or similar deliverable as a Word or .docx file, use this skill. Do NOT use for PDFs, spreadsheets, Google Docs, or general coding tasks unrelated to document generation.\n- anthropic-skills:explain-usage: Explain where this session's tokens went, with one simple chart in plain language. Use when the user says things like \"explain my usage\", \"where did my tokens go\", or asks for a usage breakdown.\n- anthropic-skills:morning: Render the user's morning brief as a styled HTML artifact, or set it up as a recurring weekday task. Use only when the user explicitly asks to run, see, or set up their morning brief, or if they invoke /morning by name. A question about their day, schedule, or calendar is not by itself a request for the brief; answer it directly instead.\n- anthropic-skills:pdf: Use this skill whenever the user wants to do anything with PDF files. This includes reading or extracting text/tables from PDFs, combining or merging multiple PDFs into one, splitting PDFs apart, rotating pages, adding watermarks, creating new PDFs, filling PDF forms, encrypting/decrypting PDFs, extracting images, and OCR on scanned PDFs to make them searchable. If the user mentions a .pdf file or asks to produce one, use this skill.\n- anthropic-skills:pptx: Use this skill any time a .pptx or .potx file is involved in any way — as input, output, or both. This includes: creating slide decks, pitch decks, or presentations; reading, parsing, or extracting text from any .pptx or .potx file (even if the extracted content will be used elsewhere, like in an email or summary); editing, modifying, or updating existing presentations; combining or splitting slide files; working with templates (.potx), layouts, speaker notes, or comments. Trigger whenever the user mentions \"deck,\" \"slides,\" \"presentation,\" or references a .pptx or .potx filename, regardless of what they plan to do with the content afterward. If a .pptx or .potx file needs to be opened, created, or touched, use this skill.\n- anthropic-skills:schedule: Create or update a scheduled task that runs automatically. Use when the user says things like \"every day\", \"each morning\", \"remind me in an hour\", \"run this at noon\", or wants to reschedule an existing task.\n- anthropic-skills:setup-cowork: Guided Cowork setup — install role-matched plugins, connect your tools, try a skill.\n- anthropic-skills:skill-creator: Create new skills, modify and improve existing skills, and measure skill performance. Use when users want to create a skill from scratch, edit, or optimize an existing skill, run evals to test a skill, benchmark skill performance with variance analysis, or optimize a skill's description for better triggering accuracy.\n- anthropic-skills:xlsx: Use this skill any time a spreadsheet file is the primary input or output. This means any task where the user wants to: open, read, edit, or fix an existing .xlsx, .xlsm, .xltx, .csv, or .tsv file (e.g., adding columns, computing formulas, formatting, charting, cleaning messy data); create a new spreadsheet from scratch or from other data sources; or convert between tabular file formats. Trigger especially when the user references a spreadsheet file by name or path — even casually (like \"the xlsx in my downloads\") — and wants something done to it or produced from it. Also trigger for cleaning or restructuring messy tabular data files (malformed rows, misplaced headers, junk data) into proper spreadsheets. The deliverable must be a spreadsheet file. Do NOT trigger when the primary deliverable is a Word document, HTML report, standalone Python script, database pipeline, or Google Sheets API integration, even if tabular data is involved.\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".\n- artifact-design: Design guidance and fundamentals for Artifacts.\n- artifact-diagramming: Diagramming know-how for Artifacts — when a picture earns its place, how to draw one that shows the real mechanism, and the inline-SVG mechanics that keep it legible in both themes.\n- artifact-capabilities: Runtime capabilities a published Artifact page can be granted — behavior static HTML cannot provide on its own, such as the page reading live or connected data, keeping state shared across viewers, or updating and republishing itself. Serves this user's live capability roster and the typed call definitions. Load it whenever the user asks for an artifact needing any such runtime behavior.\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\n- run: Launch and drive this project's app to see a change working. Use when asked to run, start, or screenshot the app, or to confirm a change works in the real app (not just tests). First looks for a project skill that already covers launching the app; otherwise falls back to built-in patterns per project type (CLI, server, TUI, Electron, browser-driven, library).\n- init: Suite has FX-PT-D2/D3, AC-26 (strict withhold), and DR-072 (lift markers) only — no generator, no P-D1 assertion. `grep FX-PT-D1 tests/` is empty.\n10\t2. BLOCKING — **Recorded arrow order not consumed for aggregation (P13/AC-08/ADR-0004).** `deriveTransmissionReductions` / `applyLifts` iterate `snapshot.arrowOrder`; `computeGraph` then folds surviving support/attack contributions after `[...groups.entries()].sort(([l],[r]) => l.localeCompare(r))` and intra-cluster contribution/`arrowId` rank. That is a second derivation of evaluation order. IEEE-754 left-fold identity is exactly why the order is recorded; literature fixtures can pass while the law fails.\n11\t3. All four gates reproduce green in my hands: `pnpm run typecheck` PASS · `pnpm run lint` PASS (27 edge rows, 0 violations; source audit `blocking: []`) · `pnpm run build` PASS · `pnpm test:s00` **20 files / 101 tests PASS** against real embedded PostgreSQL 18.4 — matching the orchestrator's 11:08 out-of-sandbox evidence exactly.\\n\\n**What is genuinely delivered.** Both S02 entry conditions are truthfully fixed, not narrated: `packages/graph/src/index.ts:338-341` is a real `LEFT JOIN LATERAL` yielding `base_strength: null` for judgement-less live nodes, and the node/arrow staleness filters now agree, so the 02 §5.5(5) forbidden state is closed at the source; `:324-380` is one client running `BEGIN ISOLATION LEVEL REPEATABLE READ READ ONLY` → both reads → `COMMIT`, with explicit rollback/release. Both are proven against the real DB at `tests/integration/graph-database.test.ts:190-210`, not only against the stub in `tests/unit/graph-snapshot.test.ts`. P2 purity is real, not claimed: `packages/propagation/package.json` admits only `kernel` + `published-arithmetic`, both themselves fenced, and I confirmed zero clock/randomness/DB/model references in the core. Operator selection runs the full P8 chain (`packages/register/src/index.ts:56-71`) with the mandatory deployment row and closed-value validation, and the supplying level lands on every number — no source literal anywhere. DR-115 holds throughout: `OPERATOR_RESOLUTION_MISSING`, `CONTINUOUS_REPLAY_SHAPE_NOT_IMPLEMENTED`, and `product([])` throwing rather than inventing an identity. AC-85 is intact — one arithmetic surface, pinned by the audit.\\n\\n**Why I am blocking anyway.** Two findings, one of them provable at the bit level.\\n\\nThe first is the ticket's own soul. P13 says every recomputation *consumes* the recorded arrow order — nobody re-derives. It doesn't. I permuted `arrowOrder` three ways over an identical graph and got a bit-identical result (`0.d751a037bb8c9` every time): the recorded order is inert for the arithmetic. What actually orders the `agg` fold is `localeCompare` over `JSON.stringify([target, polarity, \\\"singleton:\\\"+arrowId])` — an ICU-collated **opaque-id sort**, precisely what ADR-0005's ruled order exists to replace. I then held the graph and the recorded order fixed and renamed only the opaque ids: the served number moved one ulp (`…bb8c9` → `…bb8c8`). And `localeCompare` with no explicit locale resolves against the host default locale and ICU build, so the fold order — hence the last ulp — is environment-dependent across the separately-scheduled replay executable (ADR-0018 clause 2), which is the one thing manifest §4.2(a) says the recorded order exists to prevent. Reordering is genuinely ulp-significant; I found a witness (`0.9ea3306f66081` vs `0.9ea3306f6608`).\\n\\nThe second is smaller but needs V, not me. DR-071 rules the undercut's *shape* — \\\"a reduction of the targeted support edge's transmitted contribution\\\" — but no doc I can find rules whether that reduction is subtractive or multiplicative, nor the zero clamp. The code picked subtraction silently, and on the ticket's own fixture the two readings give 0.75 versus 0.78. The handoff's \\\"QUESTIONS FOR V: None\\\" is the part I can't sign.\\n\\nCLAUDE REVIEW: CHANGES REQUESTED\\n- SOLID: greenlight — frozen strategy objects behind one interface, exhaustive selection, package responsibilities clean and the dependency closure mechanically fenced.\\n- DDD: greenlight — kernel owns closed vocabulary, graph owns materialisation, register owns resolution, propagation owns pure arithmetic, ledger owns append-only receipts; no leakage across those seams.\\n- TDD: greenlight — S03-progress.log RED is a real defect (folder-removal lift), not a staged failure; fixtures are behavioral, DB-relevant ones hit real Postgres, arithmetic ones are seeded fast-check.\\n- Patterns (P-register): reject — P13 violated: the recorded arrow order is not consumed, and an ICU-collated opaque-id sort governs the fold instead (finding 1).\\n- DR-115: greenlight — no fabricated strengths anywhere; unjudged and withheld nodes emit no number, and every unimplemented shape refuses loudly.\\n- Findings:\\n  1. **BLOCKING** `packages/propagation/src/index.ts:376,381,393,414-417,425` — recorded `arrowOrder` is not consumed by the aggregation (permuting it changes nothing); fold order is `localeCompare` collation of `singleton:${arrowId}`, so (a) the served number is a function of opaque ids — renaming ids alone moved it one ulp — and (b) collation depends on host locale/ICU build, breaking DR-034 replay exactness across the separate replay executable. Fix: fold in recorded-order index, and use a code-unit comparator rather than `localeCompare` anywhere a number depends on it (`:435`, `:446` too).\\n  2. **BLOCKING** `packages/propagation/src/index.ts:389` — `Math.max(0, rawContribution - reduction)`: neither the subtractive form nor the zero clamp traces to a ruled source. DR-071 rules the shape only. Verdict-affecting (0.75 vs 0.78 on this ticket's own DR-071 fixture). Needs a V ruling plus an in-code citation; the handoff's \\\"QUESTIONS FOR V: None\\\" understates this.\\n  3. NON-BLOCKING `packages/propagation/src/index.ts:292` vs `:354,:553` — `abstained === true` is treated as unjudged for scoring and for `unjudgedNodeIds`, but `applyLifts` tests only `baseStrength === null`, so an abstained node is not transparent for OD-02 lifting. Latent (materialiseSnapshot never sets `abstained`).\\n  4. NON-BLOCKING `packages/propagation/src/index.ts:32,274` — `isFolder` has no producer: no `is_folder` column, never set by materialisation. The analysis is starting to examine how these groups are constructed within the fold.\n\nThe groups are built by iterating through node arrows that originate from the input, which are already in recorded order from the lift process. When groups are inserted into the Map, they maintain that recorded order, and then when iterating through the groups, the selected ones get pushed in that same order before being sorted by the recorded index of the surviving arrow. The sort is stable in modern JavaScript, and since each arrow has a unique index, there are no ties to worry about. The question now is whether the fold actually respects this recorded order in practice.\n\nWhen renaming opaque IDs, the grouping itself doesn't change — renaming an arrow ID only affects singleton group keys, and renaming a node ID doesn't alter which arrows target the same node. The tie-breaking for selecting the strongest member comes down to recorded order since that determines the iteration order of evaluated members, so it's deterministic and not dependent on the IDs themselves. When all children are unjudged, the complete graph still has a resolution for the root since arrows point toward it and the root itself is judged, so those arrows survive the filtering—each unjudged child contributes a null value that gets skipped in the fold, leaving just the root's tau value as the final strength.\n\nThe test uses a seeded property with 100 runs that generates arbitrary subsets of unjudged children, covering both the null tau case and the abstained boolean case. The setup matches the manifest requirement exactly: under the accumulate operator, the parent's value equals what you get from the judged children alone.\n\nI'm noting that this is a shallow single-level graph, so it doesn't exercise nested unjudged nodes where lifts would apply—that's covered separately in other tests. But the property does exercise the \"no strength row\" path non-vacuously across the runs, and it correctly avoids testing strict-and since the identity wouldn't hold there due to withholding behavior.\n\nNow checking the P13 test that verifies arrow order consumption: it constructs nodes with tiny and large values, runs aggregation in forward and reverse order, and asserts the results differ at the bit level—a real witness to order sensitivity. The renaming test preserves opaque IDs and checks the count stays unchanged, which is good, but I'm noticing the predecessor's experiment showed the groupKey sort by localeCompare was shifting the number by one ULP during grouping.\n\nThe new code groups by recordedOrderIndex instead, making each arrow a singleton, so renaming should preserve order. But the original localeCompare would sort \"large-root\" before \"tiny-a-root\" and \"tiny-b-root\", whereas the renamed opaque IDs (opaque:0, opaque:1, opaque:2) maintain the original sequence. Under the old code, renaming would diverge from the forward pass, but under the new code both follow recorded order, so the test should now pass consistently.\n\nI want to run through the bit-level experiments myself rather than relying on the test assertions. Looking at the cluster test around line 208: it has an absorbed member at index 0 and a survivor at index 3, with the survivor winning on value (0.3 > 0.1). {\n  evaluate,\n  type EvaluationSnapshot,\n  type SnapshotArrow,\n  type SnapshotNode\n} from \"@debateai/propagation\";\nimport { agg, σ } from \"@debateai/published-arithmetic\";\n\nconst bits = deterministic LCG so the probe is reproducible\nlet seed = 20260808;\nconst rnd = (): number => {\n  seed = (seed * 1103515245 + 12345) % 2147483648;\n  return seed / 2147483648;\n};\n\nconst rootOf = (s: EvaluationSnapshot): number =>\n  evaluate(s).strengths.find((r) => r.nodeId === \"root\")!.strength;\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// EXPERIMENT 1: does the fold EXACTLY follow the recorded order?\n// Reference: agg over contributions in recorded order, computed independently.\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\nconst N = 9;\nconst taus = Array.from({ length: N }, () => rnd());\nconst strengthsArr = Array.from({ length: N }, () => rnd());\nconst rootTau = 0.5;\n\nconst buildFrom = (\n  order: readonly number[],           // permutation of source indices = recorded order\n  arrowName: (i: number) => string,\n  nodeName: (i: number) => string\n): EvaluationSnapshot => {\n  const nodes: SnapshotNode[] = [\n    { nodeId: \"root\", baseStrength: rootTau },\n    ...Array.from({ length: N }, (_, i) => ({ nodeId: nodeName(i), baseStrength: taus[i]! }))\n  ];\n  const arrows: SnapshotArrow[] = Array.from({ length: N }, (_, i) => ({\n    arrowId: arrowName(i),\n    sourceNodeId: nodeName(i),\n    targetKind: \"NODE\" as const,\n    targetNodeId: \"root\",\n    targetEdgeId: null,\n    polarity: \"support\" as const,\n    kind: null,\n    strength: strengthsArr[i]!,\n    magnitudeStatus: \"MEASURED\" as const,\n    strengthSource: \"EVIDENCE_VERIFIER\" as const\n  }));\n  return {\n    nodes,\n    // deliberately shuffle the *array* so only arrowOrder can be carrying order\n    arrows: [...arrows].reverse(),\n    arrowOrder: order.map((i) => arrowName(i)),\n    operatorResolutions: [{ parentNodeId: \"root\", operator: \"accumulate\", suppliedBy: \"deployment\" }],\n    clusterRecords: []\n  };\n};\n\nconst permute = <T,>(a: readonly T[]): T[] => {\n  const out = [...a];\n  for (let i = out.length - 1; i > 0; i--) {\n    const j = Math.floor(rnd() * (i + 1));\n    [out[i], out[j]] = [out[j]!, out[i]!];\n  }\n  return out;\n};\n\nconst identity = Array.from({ length: N }, (_, i) => i);\nlet exactMatches = 0;\nlet mismatches: string[] = [];\nconst distinct = new Set<string>();\nconst TRIALS = 400;\nfor (let t = 0; t < TRIALS; t++) {\n  const order = t === 0 ? identity : permute(identity);\n  const snap = buildFrom(order, (i) => `arrow:${i}`, (i) => `src:${i}`);\n  const got = rootOf(snap);\n  // independent reference: fold contributions in recorded order\n  const contributions = order.map((i) => strengthsArr[i]! * taus[i]!);\n  const want = σ(rootTau, agg([]), agg(contributions));\n  distinct.add(bits(got));\n  if (Object.is(got, want)) exactMatches++;\n  else if (mismatches.length < 3) mismatches.push(`order=[${order}] got=${bits(got)} want=${bits(want)}`);\n}\nconsole.log(\"=== ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// EXPERIMENT 2: rename opaque ids ONLY. },\n    { arrowId: a(3), sourceNodeId: n(3), targetKind: \"NODE\", targetNodeId: \"root\", targetEdgeId: null, polarity: \"attack\", kind: \"rebutting\", strength: 0.55, magnitudeStatus: \"MEASURED\", {"parentUuid":"dee56511-4aaf-4272-a1b8-6eb35e68a6bd","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a2d6da1c26e592eaa","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011Cdq4Dw5Rxg5NRBCjUgqXk","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01PB94rCdWy6YDxiurmuvGDX","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-vladmihaimiron-Documents-DebateAI-V3/a8fcf2e3-bdd1-4a10-bcd6-3ec887012907/scratchpad/rev2-probe2.ts","content":"import {\n  evaluate,\n  type EvaluationSnapshot,\n  type SnapshotArrow,\n  type SnapshotNode\n} from \"@debateai/propagation\";\nimport { agg, σ } from \"@debateai/published-arithmetic\";\n\nconst bits = (n: number): string => {\n  const b = new ArrayBuffer(8);\n  new Float64Array(b)[0] = n;\n  return [...new Uint8Array(b)].map((x) => x.toString(16).padStart(2, \"0\")).join(\"\");\n};\n\nlet seed = 424242;\nconst rnd = (): number => { seed = (seed * 1103515245 + 12345) % 2147483648; return seed / 2147483648; };\nconst permute = <T,>(a: readonly T[]): T[] => {\n  const o = [...a];\n  for (let i = o.length - 1; i > 0; i--) { const j = Math.floor(rnd() * (i + 1)); [o[i], o[j]] = [o[j]!, o[i]!]; }\n  return o;\n};\n\nconst rootOf = (s: EvaluationSnapshot): number =>\n  evaluate(s).strengths.find((r) => r.nodeId === \"root\")!.strength;\n\n// ADVERSARIAL magnitudes: agg is a left fold of (1-v); mixing near-1 and near-0\n// makes the fold genuinely non-associative at ulp level.\nconst taus = [1 - 1e-15, 3e-17, 0.9999999999999, 7e-16, 0.5, 1e-16, 0.99999999, 2.5e-16, 0.75];\nconst N = taus.length;\nconst rootTau = 0.5;\n\nconst build = (order: readonly number[], a: (i: number) => string, n: (i: number) => string): EvaluationSnapshot => {\n  const nodes: SnapshotNode[] = [\n    { nodeId: \"root\", baseStrength: rootTau },\n    ...Array.from({ length: N }, (_, i) => ({ nodeId: n(i), baseStrength: taus[i]! }))\n  ];\n  const arrows: SnapshotArrow[] = Array.from({ length: N }, (_, i) => ({\n    arrowId: a(i), sourceNodeId: n(i), targetKind: \"NODE\" as const, targetNodeId: \"root\",\n    targetEdgeId: null, polarity: \"support\" as const, kind: null, strength: 1,\n    magnitudeStatus: \"MEASURED\" as const, strengthSource: \"EVIDENCE_VERIFIER\" as const\n  }));\n  return {\n    nodes,\n    arrows: [...arrows].reverse(),           // array order must be irrelevant\n    arrowOrder: order.map((i) => a(i)),\n    operatorResolutions: [{ parentNodeId: \"root\", operator: \"accumulate\", suppliedBy: \"deployment\" }],\n    clusterRecords: []\n  };\n};\n\nconst identity = Array.from({ length: N }, (_, i) => i);\nconst distinct = new Set<string>();\nlet exact = 0;\nconst bad: string[] = [];\nconst TRIALS = 500;\nfor (let t = 0; t < TRIALS; t++) {\n  const order = t === 0 ? identity : permute(identity);\n  const got = rootOf(build(order, (i) => `arrow:${i}`, (i) => `src:${i}`));\n  const want = σ(rootTau, agg([]), agg(order.map((i) => taus[i]!)));\n  distinct.add(bits(got));\n  if (Object.is(got, want)) exact++;\n  else if (bad.length < 3) bad.push(`order=[${order}] got=${bits(got)} want=${bits(want)}`);\n}\nconsole.log(\"=== EXP 1b: ADVERSARIAL magnitudes ===\");\nconsole.log(`  bit-exact vs independent recorded-order fold: ${exact}/${TRIALS}`);\nconsole.log(`  distinct served numbers across permutations:   ${distinct.size}  (>1 == recorded order is ulp-live)`);\nif (bad.length) console.log(\" }))\n  ],\n  arrows: Array.from({ length: N }, (_, i): SnapshotArrow => ({\n    arrowId: a(i), sourceNodeId: n(i), targetKind: \"NODE\", targetNodeId: \"root\",\n    targetEdgeId: null, polarity, kind: polarity === \"attack\" ? \"rebutting\" : null,\n    strength: 1, magnitudeStatus: \"MEASURED\", strengthSource: \"EVIDENCE_VERIFIER\"\n  })).reverse(),\n  arrowOrder: order.map((i) => a(i)),\n  operatorResolutions: [{ parentNodeId: \"root\", operator: op, suppliedBy: \"deployment\" }],\n  clusterRecords: []\n});\n\nconst identity = Array.from({ length: N }, (_, i) => i);\nconst A = (i: number) => `arrow:${i}`;\nconst NN = (i: number) => `src:${i}`;\n\nconsole.log(\"=== EXP 7: every limb, recorded-order-exact and id-inert ===\");\nfor (const [pol, op] of [[\"support\", \"accumulate\"], [\"attack\", \"accumulate\"], [\"support\", \"strict-and\"], [\"attack\", \"strict-and\"]] as const) {\n  const distinct = new Set<string>();\n  let exact = 0;\n  const bad: string[] = [];\n  const TRIALS = 400;\n  for (let t = 0; t < TRIALS; t++) {\n    const order = t === 0 ? identity : permute(identity);\n    const got = rootOf(build(order, pol, op, A, NN));\n    const vals = order.map((i) => taus[i]!);\n    const sup = pol === \"support\" ? ${exact}/${TRIALS}, distinct-across-permutations=${distinct.size}`);\n  if (bad.length) console.log(\"     MISMATCH:\", bad);\n\n  // id-inertness at a fixed recorded order, adversarial renamings\n  const ord = permute(identity);\n  const base = rootOf(build(ord, pol, op, A, NN));\n  const ren: [string, (i: number) => string, (i: number) => string][] = [\n    [\"reverse-lex\", (i) => `arrow:${99 - i}`, (i) => `src:${99 - i}`],\n    [\"diacritic\", (i) => [\"äa\", \"za\", \"Aa\", \"ab\", \"Äb\", \"zb\", \"ac\", \"Äc\", \"zc\"][i]!, (i) => `src:${i}`],\n    [\"astral\", (i) => `\\u{1F600}${i}`, folded\nconst deep = (order: readonly string[]): EvaluationSnapshot => ({\n  nodes: [\n    { nodeId: \"root\", baseStrength: 0 }, { nodeId: \"mid\", baseStrength: 0 },\n    { nodeId: \"l1\", 0003_s03.sql ===\nALTER TABLE core.node\n  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS position_label text,\n  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS is_folder boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false;\n\nALTER TABLE ledger.node_strength_record\n  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS tau_source text,\n  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS cluster_id text,\n  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS judged_by text,\n  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS abstained boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false,\n  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS supported_by jsonb NOT NULL DEFAULT '[]'::jsonb,\n  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS attacked_by jsonb NOT NULL DEFAULT '[]'::jsonb,\n  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS operator_used text,\n  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS operator_level text,\n  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS position_label text,\n  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS lift_marker jsonb NOT NULL DEFAULT '[]'::jsonb,\n  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS rival_operator text,\n  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS rival_strength double precision;\n\nDO $$\nBEGIN\n  IF NOT EXISTS (\n    SELECT 1 FROM pg_constraint\n    WHERE conname = 'node_strength_record_operator_used_check'\n      AND conrelid = 'ledger.node_strength_record'::regclass\n  ) THEN\n    ALTER TABLE ledger.node_strength_record\n      ADD CONSTRAINT node_strength_record_operator_used_check\n      CHECK (operator_used IS NULL OR operator_used IN ('accumulate', 'strict-and'));\n  END IF;\n  IF NOT EXISTS (\n    SELECT 1 FROM pg_constraint\n    WHERE conname = 'node_strength_record_operator_level_check'\n      AND conrelid = 'ledger.node_strength_record'::regclass\n  ) THEN\n    ALTER TABLE ledger.node_strength_record\n      ADD CONSTRAINT node_strength_record_operator_level_check\n      CHECK (operator_level IS NULL OR operator_level IN ('parent', 'run', 'deployment'));\n  END IF;\n  IF NOT EXISTS (\n    SELECT 1 FROM pg_constraint\n    WHERE conname = 'node_strength_record_operator_pair_check'\n      AND conrelid = 'ledger.node_strength_record'::regclass\n  ) THEN\n    ALTER TABLE ledger.node_strength_record\n      ADD CONSTRAINT node_strength_record_operator_pair_check\n      CHECK ((operator_used IS NULL) = (operator_level IS NULL));\n  END IF;\n  IF NOT EXISTS (\n    SELECT 1 FROM pg_constraint\n    WHERE conname = 'node_strength_record_rival_check'\n      AND conrelid = 'ledger.node_strength_record'::regclass\n  ) THEN\n    ALTER TABLE ledger.node_strength_record\n      ADD CONSTRAINT node_strength_record_rival_check\n      CHECK (\n        (rival_operator IS NULL AND rival_strength IS NULL)\n        OR (\n          rival_operator IS NOT NULL\n          AND rival_strength IS NOT NULL\n          AND rival_operator IN ('accumulate', 'strict-and')\n          AND rival_strength >= 0 AND rival_strength <= 1\n        )\n      );\n  END IF;\nEND $$;\n\nALTER TABLE ledger.node_strength_record\n  DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS node_strength_record_supported_by_array_check,\n  ADD CONSTRAINT node_strength_record_supported_by_array_check\n    CHECK (jsonb_typeof(supported_by) = 'array'),\n  DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS node_strength_record_attacked_by_array_check,\n  ADD CONSTRAINT node_strength_record_attacked_by_array_check\n    CHECK (jsonb_typeof(attacked_by) = 'array'),\n  DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS node_strength_record_lift_marker_array_check,\n  ADD CONSTRAINT node_strength_record_lift_marker_array_check\n    CHECK (jsonb_typeof(lift_marker) = 'array');\n\nCREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS ledger.sensitivity_record (\n  propagation_run_id uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES ledger.propagation_run(propagation_run_id),\n  removed_node_id uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES core.node(node_id),\n  leverage double precision NOT NULL CHECK (leverage >= 0 AND leverage <= 1),\n  fragility jsonb NOT NULL CHECK (jsonb_typeof(fragility) = 'array'),\n  PRIMARY KEY (propagation_run_id, removed_node_id)\n);\n\nREVOKE ALL ON ledger.sensitivity_record FROM PUBLIC;\nGRANT SELECT, INSERT ON ledger.sensitivity_record TO debateai_runtime;\nGRANT SELECT ON ledger.sensitivity_record TO debateai_replay;\nDROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS reject_mutation ON ledger.sensitivity_record;\nCREATE TRIGGER reject_mutation\n  BEFORE UPDATE OR DELETE ON ledger.sensitivity_record\n  FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION core.reject_mutation();","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"44f8462e-c3a8-4359-87f0-24b4ae6c00cd","timestamp":"2026-08-08T09:39:41.222Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"b8b6d46c-6095-45b2-bf95-b1a2313c4422","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/vladmihaimiron/Documents/DebateAI-V3","sessionId":"a8fcf2e3-bdd1-4a10-bcd6-3ec887012907","version":"2.1.221","gitBranch":"main","slug":"hidden-riding-bengio"}

Build the same graph twice with tags whose lexical\n// order is inverted, then compare the receipt SHAPES positionally.\nconst build = (tag: string, order: readonly number[]): EvaluationSnapshot => {\n  const a = (i: number) => `${tag}A${i}`;\n  const n = (i: number) => `${tag}N${i}`;\n  const ck = (g: number) => ({ evidenceProvenanceRef: `${tag}ev${g}`, producingRunId: `${tag}run${g}`, modelFamily: `${tag}fam${g}` });\n  return {\n    nodes: [\n      { nodeId: \"root\", baseStrength: 0.5 },\n      ...[0.4, 0.4, 0.6, 0.2, 0.9].map((t, i) => ({ nodeId: n(i), baseStrength: t }))\n    ],\n    arrows: [\n      { arrowId: a(0), sourceNodeId: n(0), targetKind: \"NODE\", targetNodeId: \"root\", targetEdgeId: null, polarity: \"support\", kind: null, strength: 0.5, magnitudeStatus: \"MEASURED\", strengthSource: \"EVIDENCE_VERIFIER\", clusterKey: ck(0) },\n      // exact tie with a(0): 0.5*0.4 === 0.5*0.4 -> survivor must be the FIRST recorded member\n      { arrowId: a(1), sourceNodeId: n(1), targetKind: \"NODE\", targetNodeId: \"root\", targetEdgeId: null, polarity: \"support\", kind: null, strength: 0.5, magnitudeStatus: \"MEASURED\", strengthSource: \"EVIDENCE_VERIFIER\", clusterKey: ck(0) },\n      { arrowId: a(2), sourceNodeId: n(2), targetKind: \"NODE\", targetNodeId: \"root\", targetEdgeId: null, polarity: \"attack\", kind: \"rebutting\", strength: 0.5, magnitudeStatus: \"MEASURED\", strengthSource: \"EVIDENCE_VERIFIER\" },\n      { arrowId: a(3), sourceNodeId: n(3), targetKind: \"NODE\", targetNodeId: \"root\", targetEdgeId: null, polarity: \"support\", kind: null, strength: 0.7, magnitudeStatus: \"MEASURED\", strengthSource: \"EVIDENCE_VERIFIER\" },\n      { arrowId: a(4), sourceNodeId: n(4), targetKind: \"NODE\", targetNodeId: \"root\", targetEdgeId: null, polarity: \"support\", kind: null, strength: 0.3, magnitudeStatus: \"MEASURED\", strengthSource: \"EVIDENCE_VERIFIER\" }\n    ],\n    arrowOrder: order.map((i) => a(i)),\n    operatorResolutions: [{ parentNodeId: \"root\", operator: \"accumulate\", suppliedBy: \"deployment\" }],\n    clusterRecords: []\n  };\n};\n\nconst strip = (tag: string, s: string): string => s.split(tag).join(\"<T>\");\nconst shape = (tag: string, order: readonly number[]) => {\n  const o = evaluate(build(tag, order));\n  const root = o.strengths.find((r) => r.nodeId === \"root\")!;\n  return {\n    strength: root.strength,\n    supportedBy: root.supportedBy.map((x) => strip(tag, x)),\n    attackedBy: root.attackedBy.map((x) => strip(tag, x)),\n    clusters: o.clusterRecords.map((c) => ({\n      surviving: strip(tag, c.survivingMember),\n      absorbed: c.absorbedEdgeIds.map((x) => strip(tag, x))\n    })),\n    fingerprint: strip(tag, o.graphFingerprintMaterial)\n  };\n};\n\nconst ord = [0, 1, 2, 3, 4];\nconst low = shape(\"aaa\", ord);\nconst high = shape(\"zzz\", ord);\nconsole.log(\"=== EXP 10: receipts under opaque-id renaming (lexically inverted tags) ===\");\nconsole.log(\"  strength equal:      \", Object.is(low.strength, high.strength));\nconsole.log(\"  supportedBy equal:   \", JSON.stringify(low.supportedBy) === JSON.stringify(high.supportedBy), JSON.stringify(low.supportedBy));\nconsole.log(\"  attackedBy equal:    \", JSON.stringify(low.attackedBy) === JSON.stringify(high.attackedBy));\nconsole.log(\"  clusterRecords equal:\", JSON.stringify(low.clusters) === JSON.stringify(high.clusters), JSON.stringify(low.clusters));\nconsole.log(\"  fingerprint equal:   \", low.fingerprint === high.fingerprint);\n\nconsole.log(\"\\n=== EXP 11: exact-tie cluster survivor follows recorded order, not id ===\");\nfor (const o of [[0, 1, 2, 3, 4], [1, 0, 2, 3, 4]]) {\n  const s = shape(\"aaa\", o);\n  console.log(`  recorded [${o}] -> survivor ${s.clusters[0]!.surviving}, absorbed ${JSON.stringify(s.clusters[0]!.absorbed)}`);\n}\n// and the same two orders with inverted tag: survivor must track the ORDER, not the id\nfor (const o of [[0, 1, 2, 3, 4], [1, 0, 2, 3, 4]]) {\n  const s = shape(\"zzz\", o);\n  console.log(`  (tag zzz) recorded [${o}] -> survivor ${s.clusters[0]!.surviving}`);\n}\n\nconsole.log(\"\\n=== EXP 12: receipts follow recorded order ===\");\nconst r1 = shape(\"aaa\", [0, 1, 2, 3, 4]);\nconst r2 = shape(\"aaa\", [4, 3, 2, 1, 0]);\nconsole.log(\"  order [0,1,2,3,4] supportedBy:\", JSON.stringify(r1.supportedBy));\nconsole.log(\"  order [4,3,2,1,0] supportedBy:\", JSON.stringify(r2.supportedBy));\nconsole.log(\"  supportedBy tracks recorded order:\", JSON.stringify(r1.supportedBy) !== operator: \"accumulate\"; suppliedBy: \"deployment\" }[]): EvaluationSnapshot =>\n  ({ nodes, arrows, arrowOrder: arrows.map((a) => a.arrowId), operatorResolutions: res, clusterRecords: [] });\n\nlet cases = 0, withUnjudged = 0, allUnjudged = 0, mixed = 0, totalChildren = 0, unjudgedChildren = 0;\nlet limbAchecks = 0, limbBnonTrivial = 0;\n\nfc.assert(fc.property(\n  fc.double({ min: 0, max: 1, noNaN: true }),\n  fc.array(fc.record({\n    tau: fc.option(fc.double({ min: 0, max: 1, noNaN: true }), { nil: null }),\n    abstained: fc.boolean()\n  }), { minLength: 1, maxLength: 8 }),\n  (rootTau, childFacts) => {\n    cases++;\n    const children = childFacts.map((f, i) => node(`child:${i}`, f.tau, { parentNodeId: \"root\", abstained: f.abstained }));\n    const arrows = children.map((c, i) => arrow(`edge:${i}`, c.nodeId, \"root\"));\n    const unjudged = children.filter((c) => c.baseStrength === null || c.abstained === true);\n    const judged = children.filter((c) => c.baseStrength !== null && c.abstained !== true);\n    totalChildren += children.length; unjudgedChildren += unjudged.length;\n    if (unjudged.length > 0) withUnjudged++;\n    if (unjudged.length === children.length) allUnjudged++;\n    if (unjudged.length > 0 && judged.length > 0) mixed++;\n    limbAchecks += unjudged.length;\n    if (judged.length > 1) limbBnonTrivial++;\n\n    const judgedIds = new Set(judged.map((c) => c.nodeId));\n    const complete = evaluate(snapshot([node(\"root\", rootTau), ...children], arrows, [{ parentNodeId: \"root\", operator: \"accumulate\", suppliedBy: \"deployment\" }]));\n    const judgedOnly = evaluate(snapshot(\n      [node(\"root\", rootTau), ...children.filter((c) => judgedIds.has(c.nodeId))],\n      arrows.filter((a) => judgedIds.has(a.sourceNodeId)),\n      judgedIds.size === 0 ? [] : [{ parentNodeId: \"root\", operator: \"accumulate\", suppliedBy: \"deployment\" }]\n    ));\n    const ids = new Set(complete.strengths.map((r) => r.nodeId));\n    for (const c of unjudged) if (ids.has(c.nodeId)) throw new Error(`limb A violated for ${c.nodeId}`);\n    const a = complete.strengths.find((r) => r.nodeId === \"root\")?.strength;\n    const b = judgedOnly.strengths.find((r) => r.nodeId === \"root\")?.strength;\n    if (!Object.is(a, b)) throw new Error(`limb B violated: ${a} vs ${b}`);\n  }\n), { seed: 20260808 });\n\nconsole.log(\"=== FX-PT-D1 generator reality check (seed 20260808, 100 runs) ===\");\nconsole.log(`  cases generated:                 ${cases}`);\nconsole.log(`  cases containing unjudged/abstained children: ${withUnjudged} (${Math.round(withUnjudged / cases * 100)}%)`);\nconsole.log(`  cases with ALL children unjudged: ${allUnjudged}`);\nconsole.log(`  MIXED cases (both judged + unjudged present, the load-bearing shape): ${mixed}`);\nconsole.log(`  children generated: ${totalChildren}, of which unjudged/abstained: ${unjudgedChildren}`);\nconsole.log(`  limb-A assertions actually exercised (unjudged nodes checked): Simulate the two realistic regressions the property is supposed to gate.\nconsole.log(\"\\n=== Mutation: does the property have teeth? ===\");\nconst mutate = (label: string, fn: () => void): void => {\n  try { fn(); console.log(`  ${label}: NOT CAUGHT  <-- property is vacuous here`); }\n  catch (e) { console.log(`  ${label}: CAUGHT (${(e as Error).message.slice(0, 70)})`); }\n};\n// Regression 1: unjudged child emits a strength row (fabricated tau=0)\nmutate(\"unjudged node emits a row (DR-115 fabrication)\", () => {\n  const children = [node(\"child:0\", null, { parentNodeId: \"root\", abstained: false }), node(\"child:1\", 0.4, { parentNodeId: \"root\" })];\n  const arrows = children.map((c, i) => arrow(`edge:${i}`, c.nodeId, \"root\"));\n  const out = r.nodeId));\n  if (ids.has(\"child:0\")) throw new Error(\"row emitted for unjudged child:0\");\n  // Regression 2: parent absorbs a 0-contribution from the unjudged child\n  const judgedOnly = r.nodeId === \"root\")!.strength;\n  const b = judgedOnly.strengths.find((r) => r.nodeId === \"root\")!.strength;\n  console.log(`     complete-root=${a}  judged-only-root=${b}`);\n  if (!Object.is(a, targetKind: \"NODE\", targetNodeId, targetEdgeId: null,\n  polarity: \"support\", kind: null, strength: 1, magnitudeStatus: \"MEASURED\", strengthSource: \"EVIDENCE_VERIFIER\" });\nconst snap = (nodes: Any, arrows: Any, res: Any): Any =>\n  ({ nodes, arrows, arrowOrder: arrows.map((a: Any) => a.arrowId), operatorResolutions: res, clusterRecords: [] });\nconst RES = [{ parentNodeId: \"root\", operator: \"accumulate\", suppliedBy: \"deployment\" }];\nconst results: string[] = [];\n\n// --- FX-PT-D1 (verbatim generator from tests/unit/scoring.test.ts:152) ---\ntry {\n  fc.assert(fc.property(\n    fc.double({ min: 0, max: 1, noNaN: true }),\n    fc.array(fc.record({ tau: fc.option(fc.double({ min: 0, max: 1, noNaN: true }), { nil: null }), abstained: fc.boolean() }), { minLength: 1, maxLength: 8 }),\n    (rootTau: number, childFacts: Any[]) => {\n      const children = childFacts.map((f, i) => node(`child:${i}`, f.tau, { parentNodeId: \"root\", abstained: f.abstained }));\n      const arrows = children.map((c, i) => arrow(`edge:${i}`, c.nodeId, \"root\"));\n      const complete = evaluate(snap([node(\"root\", rootTau), ...children], arrows, RES));\n      const judgedIds = new Set(children.filter((c) => c.baseStrength !== null && c.abstained !== true).map((c) => c.nodeId));\n      const judgedOnly = evaluate(snap([node(\"root\", rootTau), ...children.filter((c) => judgedIds.has(c.nodeId))],\n        arrows.filter((a: Any) => judgedIds.has(a.sourceNodeId)), judgedIds.size === 0 ? [] : RES));\n      const ids = new Set(complete.strengths.map((r: Any) => r.nodeId));\n      for (const c of children) if (c.baseStrength === null || c.abstained === true) { if (ids.has(c.nodeId)) throw new Error(\"limb A: row for unjudged\"); }\n      const a = complete.strengths.find((r: Any) => r.nodeId === \"root\")?.strength;\n      const b = judgedOnly.strengths.find((r: Any) => r.nodeId === \"root\")?.strength;\n      if (!Object.is(a, b)) throw new Error(`limb B: ${a} vs ${b}`);\n    }), { seed: 20260808 });\n  results.push(\"FX-PT-D1: PASS\");\n} catch (e) { results.push(`FX-PT-D1: FAIL (${String((e as Error).message).split(\"\\n\")[0]!.slice(0, 60)})`); }\n\n// --- P13 recorded-order witness (verbatim from scoring.test.ts:184) ---\ntry {\n  const nodes = [node(\"root\", 0), node(\"tiny-a\", 1e-16), node(\"tiny-b\", 1e-16), node(\"large\", 0.3)];\n  const arrows = [arrow(\"tiny-a-root\", \"tiny-a\", \"root\"), arrow(\"tiny-b-root\", \"tiny-b\", \"root\"), arrow(\"large-root\", \"large\", \"root\")];\n  const fwd = snap(nodes, arrows, RES);\n  const rev = { ...fwd, arrowOrder: [...fwd.arrowOrder].reverse() };\n  const f = evaluate(fwd).strengths.find((r: Any) => r.nodeId === \"root\")!.strength;\n  const r = evaluate(rev).strengths.find((r2: Any) => r2.nodeId === \"root\")!.strength;\n  if (!Object.is(f, agg([1e-16, 1e-16, 0.3]))) throw new Error(\"forward != recorded-order fold\");\n  if (!Object.is(r, agg([0.3, 1e-16, 1e-16]))) throw new Error(\"reverse != recorded-order fold\");\n  if (Object.is(f, r)) throw new Error(\"recorded order inert\");\n  results.push(\"P13 recorded-order: PASS\");\n} catch (e) { results.push(`P13 recorded-order: FAIL (${String((e as Error).message).slice(0, 60)})`); }\n\n// --- DR-071/DR-127 0.75 ---\ntry {\n  const support: Any = { arrowId: \"support\", sourceNodeId: \"source\", targetKind: \"NODE\", targetNodeId: \"root\", targetEdgeId: null, polarity: \"support\", kind: null, strength: 0.8, magnitudeStatus: \"MEASURED\", strengthSource: \"EVIDENCE_VERIFIER\" };\n  const undercut: Any = { arrowId: \"undercut\", sourceNodeId: \"root\", targetKind: \"EDGE\", targetNodeId: null, targetEdgeId: \"support\", polarity: \"attack\", kind: \"undercutting\", strength: 0.3, magnitudeStatus: \"MEASURED\", strengthSource: \"UNDERCUT_TRANSMISSION\" };\n  const out = evaluate(snap([node(\"root\", 0.5), node(\"source\", 1)], [support, undercut], RES));\n  const v = out.strengths.find((r: Any) => r.nodeId === \"root\")?.strength;\n  if (v !== 0.75) throw new Error(`served ${v}, expected 0.75`);\n  results.push(\"DR-071/DR-127 0.75: PASS\");\n} catch (e) { results.push(`DR-071/DR-127 0.75: FAIL (${String((e as Error).message).slice(0, 60)})`); }))\n    ],\n    arrows,\n    arrowOrder: order.map((s) => `${tag}${s}`),\n    operatorResolutions: [{ parentNodeId: \"root\", operator: \"accumulate\", suppliedBy: \"deployment\" }],\n    clusterRecords: []\n  };\n};\n\nconsole.log(\"=== EXP 13: multi-undercut reduction accumulation (+=) ===\");\nconst fwd = [\"support\", \"uc0\", \"uc1\", \"uc2\", \"uc3\", \"uc4\"];\nconst rev = [\"support\", \"uc4\", \"uc3\", \"uc2\", \"uc1\", \"uc0\"];\nconst a = rootOf(build(\"aaa\", fwd));\nconst b = rootOf(build(\"aaa\", rev));\nconsole.log(`  recorded uc0..uc4 -> ${bits(a)} sumFwd))}`);\nconsole.log(`  rev matches left-fold sum in recorded order: ${Object.is(b, 0.5 + 0.5 * Math.max(0, 1 - sumRev))}`);\nconsole.log(`  id-inert (tag aaa vs zzz, same order): ${Object.is(rootOf(build(\"zzz\", fwd)), a)}`);\n\nconsole.log(\"\\n=== EXP 14: clusterRecords / arrowOrder receipt determinism across repeated calls ===\");\nconst s = build(\"aaa\", fwd);\nconst r1
